detail = f\"claude {_format_version(parsed)}\"\n180\t    if not passed:\n181\t        detail += f\" < required {_format_version(min_version)}\"\n182\t    return CapabilityStatus(\n183\t _check_claude_home(home: Optional[Path] = None) -> CapabilityStatus:\n192\t    \"\"\"HARD check: ``~/.claude/`` (or override) exists and is a directory.\"\"\"\n193\t    target = home if home is not ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n205\t# NFR-PERF2 free-RAM precheck (T03.17 / D-0059)\n206\t# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n207\t#\n208\t# Design-spec §11 (NFR-PERF2) pins peak resident-set-size at 2.25 GB for\n209\t# ``--parallel 15``. When an operator requests the maximum concurrency on a\n210\t# host that cannot afford that ceiling, the doctor MUST emit a warning\n211\t# string containing the literal ``\"2.25 GB\"`` token so the operator can\n212\t# tell at a glance which budget was insufficient. Hosts with less than\n213\t# 4 GB free RAM are out-of-scope per ``CLAUDE.md`` infrastructure notes —\n214\t# the warning is the documented stop-gap, not an exit-2 hard failure\n215\t# (which would block legitimate ``--parallel 8`` runs on tight hosts).\n216\t\n217\tRAM_CEILING_GB: float = 2.25\n218\t\"\"\"Design-spec §11 NFR-PERF2 peak-RSS ceiling at ``--parallel 15``.\n219\t\n220\tThe literal value ``2.25`` (not the bytes) is the one operators see in\n221\tthe warning text, so changing it requires a roadmap edit. The bytes\n222\tconstant below is derived from it so the two views cannot drift.\n223\t\"\"\"\n224\t\n225\tRAM_CEILING_BYTES: int = int(RAM_CEILING_GB * (1024**3))\n226\t\"\"\"Byte-equivalent of :data:`RAM_CEILING_GB` for free-RAM comparisons.\n227\t\n228\t``int(2.25 * 1024**3) == 2_415_919_104``. Computed once at import time so\n229\tthe helper does not re-derive it on every call.\n230\t\"\"\"\n231\t\n232\tRAM_CEILING_TEXT: str = f\"{RAM_CEILING_GB} GB\"\n233\t\"\"\"Canonical rendering of the ceiling used in operator-facing strings.\n234\t\n235\tThe T03.17 AC asserts that the warning contains this literal token. Pin\n236\tit here so any downstream string formatting picks up changes uniformly.\n237\t\"\"\"\n238\t\n239\tPARALLEL_RAM_GATE_THRESHOLD: int = 15\n240\t\"\"\"``--parallel`` value at-or-above which the RAM precheck fires.\n241\t\n242\tMatches :attr:`RunOrchestrator.MAX_PARALLEL`. Below this threshold the\n243\tbenchmark does not document a ceiling, so the doctor stays silent rather\n244\tthan emit a misleading number.\n245\t\"\"\"\n246\t\n247\t\n248\tdef _default_free_ram_probe() -> Optional[int]:\n249\t    \"\"\"Return free RAM in bytes via Linux ``/proc/meminfo``.\n250\t\n251\t    Prefers ``MemAvailable`` (kernel ≥3.14) because it accounts for\n252\t    reclaimable cache the way an oncoming allocation would; falls back to\n253\t    ``MemFree`` on ancient kernels. Returns ``None`` on any failure (eg\n254\t    non-Linux host, locked-down container) so the doctor records a\n255\t    ``probe unavailable`` SOFT-SKIP rather than crashing — a doctor that\n256\t    crashes on tight hosts is worse than one that warns.\n257\t    \"\"\"\n258\t\n259\t    meminfo = Path(\"/proc/meminfo\")\n260\t    if not meminfo.is_file():\n261\t        return None\n262\t    try:\n263\t        text = meminfo.read_text(encoding=\"utf-8\")\n264\t    except OSError:\n265\t        return None\n266\t    available: Optional[int] = None\n267\t    free: Optional[int] = None\n268\t    for line in text.splitlines():\n269\t        key, _, rest = line.partition(\":\")\n270\t        key = key.strip()\n271\t        if key not in (\"MemAvailable\", \"MemFree\"):\n272\t            continue\n273\t        tokens = rest.strip().split()\n274\t        if not tokens:\n275\t            continue\n276\t        try:\n277\t            kb = int(tokens[0])\n278\t        except ValueError:\n279\t            continue\n280\t        unit = tokens[1].lower() if len(tokens) > 1 else \"kb\"\n281\t        # /proc/meminfo always reports in kB on Linux; the unit check is\n282\t        # defensive in case a future kernel changes the format.\n283\t        multiplier = 1024 if unit == \"kb\" else 1\n284\t        value = kb * multiplier\n285\t        if key == \"MemAvailable\":\n286\t            available = value\n287\t        elif key == \"MemFree\":\n288\t            free = value\n289\t    if available is not None:\n290\t        return available\n291\t    return free\n292\t\n293\t\n294\tdef _check_free_ram_for_parallel(\n295\t    *,\n296\t    requested_parallel: Optional[int],\n297\t    probe: Optional[Callable[[], Optional[int]]] = None,\n298\t    ceiling_bytes: int = RAM_CEILING_BYTES,\n299\t    threshold_parallel: int = PARALLEL_RAM_GATE_THRESHOLD,\n300\t) -> Optional[CapabilityStatus]:\n301\t    \"\"\"SOFT-SKIP check: free RAM is sufficient for the requested concurrency.\n302\t\n303\t    Returns ``None`` (gate inactive) when ``requested_parallel`` is below\n304\t    :data:`PARALLEL_RAM_GATE_THRESHOLD` — the design-spec only pins the\n305\t    RSS ceiling for the upper-bound run, so warning operators about\n306\t    insufficient RAM at ``--parallel 8`` would be noise.\n307\t\n308\t    When the gate is active the helper returns a :class:`CapabilityStatus`\n309\t    whose ``failure_mode`` is ``\"skip\"`` regardless of outcome:\n310\t\n311\t    * Passed when the probe returns a value at or above\n312\t      :data:`RAM_CEILING_BYTES`.\n313\t    * Failed (SOFT-SKIP) when free RAM is below the ceiling, with the\n314\t      detail string containing the literal :data:`RAM_CEILING_TEXT` token\n315\t      so a grep against the operator-facing artifact pins the failure\n316\t      mode without parsing structure.\n317\t    * Failed (SOFT-SKIP) when the probe returned ``None`` — the host\n318\t      cannot self-report free RAM so we cannot certify the budget either.\n319\t    \"\"\"\n320\t\n321\t    if requested_parallel is None or requested_parallel < threshold_parallel:\n322\t        return None\n323\t\n324\t    probe = probe or _default_free_ram_probe\n325\t    description = (\n326\t        f\"free RAM >= {RAM_CEILING_TEXT} for --parallel {threshold_parallel}\"\n327\t    )\n328\t    try:\n329\t        free_ram = probe()\n330\t    except Exception as exc:  # noqa: BLE001 — probe is caller-supplied\n331\t        return CapabilityStatus(\n332\t            name=\"host.free_ram_for_max_parallel\",\n333\t            passed=False,\n334\t            failure_mode=\"skip\",\n335\t            description=description,\n336\t            detail=(\n337\t                f\"free-RAM probe raised {type(exc).__name__}: {exc}; \"\n338\t                f\"{RAM_CEILING_TEXT} budget unverified\"\n339\t            ),\n340\t        )\n341\t    if free_ram is None:\n342\t        return CapabilityStatus(\n343\t            name=\"host.free_ram_for_max_parallel\",\n344\t            passed=False,\n345\t            failure_mode=\"skip\",\n346\t            description=description,\n347\t            detail=(\n348\t                f\"free-RAM probe unavailable on this host; cannot verify \"\n349\t                f\"{RAM_CEILING_TEXT} budget\"\n350\t            ),\n351\t        )\n352\t    free_gb = free_ram / (1024**3)\n353\t    if free_ram < ceiling_bytes:\n354\t        return CapabilityStatus(\n355\t            name=\"host.free_ram_for_max_parallel\",\n356\t            passed=False,\n357\t            failure_mode=\"skip\",\n358\t            description=description,\n359\t            detail=(\n360\t                f\"free RAM {free_gb:.2f} GB < {RAM_CEILING_TEXT} ceiling \"\n361\t                f\"for --parallel {threshold_parallel}\"\n362\t            ),\n363\t        )\n364\t    return CapabilityStatus(\n365\t        name=\"host.free_ram_for_max_parallel\",\n366\t        passed=True,\n367\t        failure_mode=\"skip\",\n368\t        description=description,\n369\t        detail=f\"free RAM {free_gb:.2f} GB >= {RAM_CEILING_TEXT}\",\n370\t    )\n371\t\n372\t\n373\tdef _check_ptytest_vendored(pty_dir: Optional[Path] = None) -> CapabilityStatus:\n374\t    \"\"\"SOFT-SKIP \"\"\"Render the stderr HARD-failure artifact per FR-CLI4 AC.\"\"\"\n515\t    by_name = {row.name: row for row in report.report}\n516\t    lines = [\"eval doctor: HARD failures\"]\n517\t    for name ``coverage_gate``\n535\t    marker so callers can detect whether ``--check-coverage`` was passed.\n536\t    The base ``CapabilityReport`` keys are preserved verbatim so the\n537\t    payload remains Re-exports\n579\t:data:`signal_handler.EXIT_INTERRUPTED` (= 3) so the two surfaces cannot\n580\tdrift.\"\"\"\n581\t\n582\t\n583\t# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n584\t# AC1 / R-109 / T06.07 — Linux-only v1 platform precheck\n585\t# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n586\t#\n587\t# Roadmap row 353 (AC1 / R-109) pins v1 to Linux. The doctor refuses any\n588\t# other ``platform.system()`` value with a friendly stderr message before\n589\t# any capability gates run, so a macOS or Windows operator gets a single\n590\t# actionable line instead of a cascade of HARD failures triggered by\n591\t# absent ``/proc/meminfo``, missing ``jq``, etc. Re-uses\n592\t# :data:`HARD_FAIL_EXIT_CODE` (2) because a non-Linux host is a precondition\n593\t# failure of the same class as a missing ``claude`` binary.\n594\t\n595\tNON_LINUX_REFUSAL_TEMPLATE: str = (\n596\t    \"eval doctor: unsupported platform: {system!r}. \"\n597\t    \"The cliEval harness ships Linux-only for v1 (AC1, roadmap R-109). \"\n598\t    \"macOS support is deferred to v2 — see DOC-OQ9 closure in \"\n599\t    \".dev/releases/current/cliEval/decisions.md.\"\n600\t)\n601\t\"\"\"Friendly non-Linux refusal message rendered to stderr by ``eval doctor``.\n602\t\n603\tThe template cites AC1 and DOC-OQ9 so an operator landing here can trace\n604\tthe v1 platform commitment back to the ADR log without a documentation\n605\thunt. Pin as a module-level constant so tests can match on it without\n606\tduplicating the string.\n607\t\"\"\"\n608\t\n609\t\n610\tdef _default_platform_probe() -> str:\n611\t    \"\"\"Return :func:`platform.system` output.\n612\t\n613\t    Wrapped so tests can monkey-patch the doctor's platform view\n614\t    independently of the host they run on (the cliEval suite is\n615\t    Linux-only at v1 ship, but unit tests must still exercise the\n616\t    macOS/Windows refusal branch on a Linux CI box).\n617\t    \"\"\"\n618\t    return platform.system()\n619\t\n620\t\n621\tdef _format_coverage_summary(result: CoverageResult) -> str:\n622\t    \"\"\"One-line human summary for ``doctor --check-coverage`` stdout.\"\"\"\n623\t    if not result.matchers:\n624\t        return \"coverage gate: no in-scope matchers (passed)\"\n625\t    if result.passed:\n626\t        return (\n627\t            f\"coverage gate: {len(result.covered)}/{len(result.matchers)} \"\n628\t            f\"matcher(s) covered (passed)\"\n629\t        )\n630\t    return (\n631\t        f\"coverage gate: {len(result.missing)} of {len(result.matchers)} \"\n632\t        f\"matcher(s) uncovered (FAILED)\"\n633\t    )\n634\t\n635\t\n636\tdef _format_coverage_missing_roster(result: CoverageResult) -> str:\n637\t    \"\"\"Render the stderr artifact when the coverage gate fails.\"\"\"\n638\t    lines = [\"eval doctor: coverage gate FAILED — uncovered matcher patterns:\"]\n639\t    for m in result.missing:\n640\t        lines.append(f\"  - {m.event}: {m.pattern}\")\n641\t    return \"\\n\".join(lines)\n642\t\n643\t\n644\t# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n645\t# FR-CLI2 ``eval list`` (T01.21 / D-0018)\n646\t# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n647\t\n648\t\n649\t_DEFAULT_SUITES_DIR: Path = SCHEMA_PATH.parent\n650\t\"\"\"Default location for built-in suite manifests.\n651\t\n652\tResolves to ``src/superclaude/cli/eval/suites/``. The glob in\n655\t:func:`discover_suite_manifests` filters to ``*.yaml`` so the schema and\n656\tpackage marker never appear in the listing.\n657\t\"\"\"\n658\t\n659\t\n660\tdef discover_suite_manifests(suites_dir: Path) -> list[Path]:\n661\t    \"\"\"Return ``suites_dir/*.yaml`` sorted by filename for determinism.\n662\t\n663\t    ``eval list`` MUST produce identical output for a given suite\n664\t    directory across invocations (FR-CLI2 AC), so the sort is part of the\n665\t    contract — not an implementation detail.\n666\t\n667\t    A missing or non-directory ``suites_dir`` is treated as the\n668\t    empty-directory case (no suites found) rather than an error: a fresh\n669\t    checkout that has not yet populated built-in suites must not break\n670\t    ``eval list``.\n671\t    \"\"\"\n672\t\n673\t    if not suites_dir.is_dir():\n674\t        return []\n675\t    return sorted(suites_dir.glob(\"*.yaml\"))\n676\t\n677\t\n678\t@dataclass(frozen=True)\n679\tclass SuiteSummary:\n680\t    \"\"\"One row in the ``eval list`` output.\n681\t\n682\t    Attributes:\n683\t        name: Manifest ``name`` field (verbatim).\n684\t        version: Manifest ``version`` field (verbatim, string).\n685\t        eval_count: Number of evals after parameterize expansion. \"\"\"Project a :class:`ParsedSuite` into the manifest envelope shape.\n1068\t\n1069\t    ``evals`` is the post-parameterize-expansion list (the canonical\n1070\t    \"what would run if you invoked Path) -> Path:\n1088\t    \"\"\"Resolve ``--suite <token>`` to a manifest path on disk.\n1089\t\n1090\t    Resolution precedence (first match wins):\n1091\t\n1092\t    1. ``token`` is itself a file path that exists → return it.\n1093\t    2. ``suites_dir / f\"{token}.yaml\"`` exists → return it (filename stem\n1094\t       convention used by the built-in suites).\n1095\t    3. Otherwise scan ``suites_dir`` and return the first manifest whose\n1096\t       schema-validated ``name`` field equals ``token``. Broken manifests\n1097\t       in the same directory are skipped silently — we only fail on the\n1098\t       *requested* suite, not on the neighbours.\n1099\t\n1100\t    Raises:\n1101\t        SuiteNotFound: No manifest matched the token under any rule. CLI\n1102\t            callers map this to :data:`SUITE_NOT_FOUND_EXIT_CODE` (= 2).\n1103\t    \"\"\"\n1104\t\n1105\t    candidate = Path(suite)\n1106\t    if candidate.is_file():\n1107\t        return candidate\n1108\t\n1109\t    by_filename = suites_dir / f\"{suite}.yaml\"\n1110\t    if by_filename.is_file():\n1111\t        return by_filename\n1112\t\n1113\t    loader = SuiteLoader()\n1114\t    for manifest_path in discover_suite_manifests(suites_dir):\n1115\t        try:\n1116\t            parsed = loader.load(manifest_path)\n1117\t        except SuiteLoaderError:\n1118\t            # Neighbour manifest is broken; don't let it mask the lookup.\n1119\t            continue\n1120\t        if parsed.name == suite:\n1121\t            return manifest_path\n1122\t\n1123\t    raise SuiteNotFound(suite, suites_dir)\n1124\t\n1125\t\n1126\tdef describe_suite(\n1127\t    suite: str,\n1128\t    *,\n1129\t    suites_dir: Path,\n1130\t    eval_id: Optional[str] = None,\n1131\t    loader: Optional[SuiteLoader] = None,\n1132\t) -> dict:\n1133\t    \"\"\"Resolve, load, and project a suite (optionally a single eval).\n1134\t\n1135\t    Validation runs via :class:`SuiteLoader` BEFORE any rendering, so\n1136\t    schema / id-regex / capability rejections raise before the caller\n1137\t    can produce stdout (FR-CLI3 AC: validate before print).\n1138\t\n1139\t    Args:\n1140\t        suite: Suite name, filename stem, or direct manifest path. See\n1141\t            :func:`resolve_suite_manifest` for resolution precedence.\n1142\t        suites_dir: Directory to search when ``suite`` is not a direct\n1143\t            path. Tests inject a tmp dir via ``--suites-dir``.\n1144\t        eval_id: Optional post-expansion eval id (``E1``, ``E2.1``) to\n1145\t            filter the output to a single eval. When an operator requests the maximum concurrency on a\n# host that cannot afford that ceiling, the doctor MUST emit a warning\n# string containing the literal ``\"2.25 GB\"`` token so the operator can\n# tell at a glance which budget was insufficient. The bytes\nconstant below is derived from it so the two views cannot drift.\n\"\"\"\n\nRAM_CEILING_BYTES: int = int(RAM_CEILING_GB * (1024**3))\n\"\"\"Byte-equivalent of :data:`RAM_CEILING_GB` for free-RAM comparisons.\n\n``int(2.25 * 1024**3) == 2_415_919_104``. Returns ``None`` on any failure (eg\n    non-Linux host, locked-down container) so the doctor records a\n    ``probe unavailable`` SOFT-SKIP rather than crashing — a doctor that\n    crashes on tight hosts is worse than one that warns.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    meminfo = Path(\"/proc/meminfo\")\n    if not meminfo.is_file():\n        return None\n    try:\n        text = meminfo.read_text(encoding=\"utf-8\")\n    except OSError:\n        return None\n    available: Optional[int] = None\n    free: Optional[int] = None\n    for line in text.splitlines():\n        key, _, rest = line.partition(\":\")\n        key = key.strip()\n        if key not in (\"MemAvailable\", \"MemFree\"):\n            continue\n        tokens = rest.strip().split()\n        if not tokens:\n            continue\n        try:\n            kb = int(tokens[0])\n        except ValueError:\n            continue\n        unit = tokens[1].lower() if len(tokens) > 1 else \"kb\"\n        # /proc/meminfo always reports in kB on Linux; the unit check is\n        # defensive in case a future kernel changes the format.\n        multiplier = 1024 if unit == \"kb\" else 1\n        value = kb * multiplier\n        if key == \"MemAvailable\":\n            available = value\n        elif key == \"MemFree\":\n            free = value\n    if available is not None:\n        return available\n    return free\n\n\ndef _check_free_ram_for_parallel(\n    *,\n    requested_parallel: Optional[int],\n    probe: Optional[Callable[[], Optional[int]]] = None,\n    ceiling_bytes: int = tuple[str, str]:\n    \"\"\"Return ``(glyph, tag)`` for the human checklist line.\"\"\"\n    if row.passed:\n        return \"[ok]\", \"\"\n    if row.skipped_by_flag:\n        return \"[--]\", \" \" (xfail)\"\n\n\ndef render_checklist(report: CapabilityReport) -> str:\n    \"\"\"Render the doctor green-checklist text (FR-CLI4 human output).\"\"\"\n    lines = [\"superclaude eval doctor:\"]\n AC.\"\"\"\n    by_name = {row.name: row for row in report.report}\n    lines = [\"eval doctor: HARD failures\"]\n    for name in report.hard_failures:\n        row = by_name.get(name)\n        if row is None:\n            lines.append(f\"  - {name}\")\n            continue\n        lines.append(f\"  - {name} ({row.description}): {row.detail}\")\n    return \"\\n\".join(lines)\n\n\ndef doctor_payload(\n    report: CapabilityReport,\n    *,\n    check_coverage: bool,\n    coverage_result: Optional[CoverageResult] = None,\n) -> dict:\n    \"\"\"Build the ``--json`` payload.\n\n    Extends :meth:`CapabilityReport.to_json` with a ``coverage_gate``\n    marker so callers can detect whether ``--check-coverage`` was passed.\n    The base ``CapabilityReport`` keys are preserved verbatim so the\n    payload remains compatible with the D-0010 contract.\n\n    When ``check_coverage`` is on and ``coverage_result`` is supplied\n    (T04.14 wiring), the marker carries the full :meth:`CoverageResult.to_dict`\n    payload under ``result``; ``status`` is ``\"passed\"`` / ``\"failed\"``\n    based on ``coverage_result.passed``. When ``check_coverage`` is on\n    but no suite was supplied the gate runs in \"matchers-only\" mode\n    (no covering evals to resolve), so ``coverage_result`` is still\n    populated but ``coverage_map`` will be empty.\n    \"\"\"\n    payload = report.to_json()\n    marker: dict[str, object] = {\"requested\": bool(check_coverage)}\n    if check_coverage and coverage_result is not None:\n        marker[\"status\"] = \"passed\" if coverage_result.passed else \"failed\"\n        marker[\"result\"] = coverage_result.to_dict()\n    else:\n        marker[\"status\"] = \"skipped\"\n    payload[\"coverage_gate\"] = marker\n    return payload\n\n\nHARD_FAIL_EXIT_CODE = 2\n\n\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n# Design-spec §4 exit-code constants for ``eval run``\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n#\n# Pinned by tests/cli/eval/test_exit_codes.py (TEST-008 / T04.19 / D-0079).\n# The interrupted code is re-exported from ``signal_handler.EXIT_INTERRUPTED``\n# so the value has exactly one source of truth — operators and the signal\n# handler both read the same integer.\n\nRUN_CLEAN_EXIT_CODE: int = 0\n\"\"\"Clean run: every expanded eval reached PASS / SKIPPED / XFAIL and no breach.\"\"\"\n\nRUN_FAILURES_EXIT_CODE: int = 1\n\"\"\"Failing run: at least one eval ended FAIL / ERRORED / TIMEOUT / XPASS but\nthe harness ran to completion.\"\"\"\n\nRUN_INTERRUPTED_EXIT_CODE: int = EXIT_INTERRUPTED\n\"\"\"SIGINT / SIGTERM mid-run; cooperative cancellation drained. Re-exports\n:data:`signal_handler.EXIT_INTERRUPTED` (= 3) so the two surfaces cannot\ndrift.\"\"\"\n\n\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n# AC1 / R-109 / T06.07 — Linux-only v1 platform precheck\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n#\n# Roadmap row 353 (AC1 / R-109) pins v1 to Linux. The doctor refuses any\n# other ``platform.system()`` value with a friendly stderr message before\n# any capability gates run, so a macOS or Windows operator gets a single\n# actionable line instead of a cascade of HARD failures triggered by\n# absent ``/proc/meminfo``, missing ``jq``, etc. Re-uses\n# :data:`HARD_FAIL_EXIT_CODE` (2) because a non-Linux host is a precondition\n# failure of the same class as a missing ``claude`` binary.\n\nNON_LINUX_REFUSAL_TEMPLATE: str = (\n    \"eval doctor: unsupported platform: {system!r}. \"\n    \"The cliEval harness ships Linux-only for v1 (AC1, roadmap R-109). \"\n    \"macOS support is deferred to v2 — see DOC-OQ9 closure in \"\n    \".dev/releases/current/cliEval/decisions.md.\"\n)\n\"\"\"Friendly non-Linux refusal message rendered to stderr by ``eval doctor``.\n\nThe template cites AC1 and DOC-OQ9 so an operator landing here can trace\nthe v1 platform commitment back to the ADR log without a documentation\nhunt. Pin as a module-level constant so tests can match on it without\nduplicating the string.\n\"\"\"\n\n\ndef _default_platform_probe() -> str:\n    \"\"\"Return :func:`platform.system` output.\n\n    Wrapped so tests can monkey-patch the doctor's platform view\n    independently of the host they run on (the cliEval suite is\n    Linux-only at v1 ship, but unit tests must still exercise the\n    macOS/Windows refusal branch on a Linux CI box).\n    \"\"\"\n    return platform.system()\n\n\ndef _format_coverage_summary(result: CoverageResult) -> str:\n    \"\"\"One-line human summary for ``doctor --check-coverage`` stdout.\"\"\"\n    if not result.matchers:\n        return \"coverage gate: no in-scope matchers (passed)\"\n    if result.passed:\n        return (\n            f\"coverage gate: {len(result.covered)}/{len(result.matchers)} \"\n            f\"matcher(s) covered (passed)\"\n        )\n    return (\n        f\"coverage gate: {len(result.missing)} of {len(result.matchers)} \"\n        f\"matcher(s) uncovered (FAILED)\"\n    )\n\n\ndef _format_coverage_missing_roster(result: CoverageResult) -> str:\n    \"\"\"Render the stderr artifact when the coverage gate fails.\"\"\"\n    lines = [\"eval doctor: coverage gate FAILED — uncovered matcher patterns:\"]\n    for m in result.missing:\n        lines.append(f\"  - {m.event}: {m.pattern}\")\n    return \"\\n\".join(lines)\n\n\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n# FR-CLI2 ``eval list`` (T01.21 / D-0018)\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n\n\n_DEFAULT_SUITES_DIR: Path = SCHEMA_PATH.parent\n\"\"\"Default location for built-in suite manifests.\n\nResolves to ``src/superclaude/cli/eval/suites/``. The glob in\n:func:`discover_suite_manifests` filters to ``*.yaml`` so the schema and\npackage marker never appear in the listing.\n\"\"\"\n\n\ndef discover_suite_manifests(suites_dir: Path) -> list[Path]:\n    \"\"\"Return ``suites_dir/*.yaml`` sorted by filename for determinism.\n\n    ``eval list`` MUST produce identical output for a given suite\n    directory across invocations (FR-CLI2 AC), so the sort is part of the\n    contract — not an implementation detail.\n\n    A missing or non-directory ``suites_dir`` is treated as the\n    empty-directory case (no suites found) rather than an error: a fresh\n    checkout that has not yet populated built-in suites must not break\n    ``eval list``.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    if not suites_dir.is_dir():\n        return []\n    return sorted(suites_dir.glob(\"*.yaml\"))\n\n\n@dataclass(frozen=True)\nclass SuiteSummary:\n    \"\"\"One row in the ``eval list`` output.\n\n    Attributes:\n        name: Manifest ``name`` field (verbatim).\n        version: Manifest ``version`` field (verbatim, string).\n        eval_count: Number of evals after parameterize expansion. _evalspec_to_dict(spec: EvalSpec) -> dict:\n    \"\"\"Project an ``EvalSpec`` into a manifest-shaped dict.\n\n    Field ordering matches ``suite.schema.json`` so YAML/JSON output is\n    deterministic and round-trips visually against the source manifest.\n    Optional fields are omitted when they hold their dataclass default\n    (e.g. ``category=\"\"``, empty tuples, ``timeout_sec=None``) so the\n    \"no editorial transformation\" guarantee from T01.22 Notes holds: an\n    eval that did not declare ``category:`` in YAML does not gain one\n    here.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    payload: dict[str, Any] = {\n        \"id\": spec.id,\n        \"title\": spec.title,\n    }\n    if spec.category:\n        payload[\"category\"] = spec.category\n    if spec.requires:\n        payload[\"requires\"] = list(spec.requires)\n    if spec.timeout_sec is not None:\n        payload[\"timeout_sec\"] = spec.timeout_sec\n    if spec.isolation is not None:\n        payload[\"isolation\"] = dict(spec.isolation)\n    if spec.inputs:\n        payload[\"inputs\"] = [dict(row) for row in spec.inputs]\n    if spec.expects:\n        payload[\"expects\"] = [dict(row) for row in spec.expects]\n    if spec.parameterize:\n        payload[\"parameterize\"] = [dict(row) for row in spec.parameterize]\n    if spec.no_pty is not None:\n        payload[\"no_pty\"] = spec.no_pty\n    return payload\n\n\ndef _parsed_suite_to_dict(parsed: ParsedSuite) -> dict:\n    \"\"\"Project a :class:`ParsedSuite` into the manifest envelope shape.\n\n    ``evals`` is the post-parameterize-expansion list (the canonical\n    \"what would run if you invoked ``eval run``\" view per FR-CLI3 AC).\n    Suite-level fields are verbatim from the manifest so the output\n    mirrors the validated source byte-for-byte modulo the ``evals``\n    expansion.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    return {\n        \"name\": parsed.name,\n        \"version\": parsed.version,\n        \"description\": parsed.description,\n        \"defaults\": dict(parsed.defaults),\n        \"required_binaries\": [dict(row) for row in parsed.required_binaries],\n        \"optional_capabilities\": [dict(row) for row in parsed.optional_capabilities],\n        \"evals\": [_evalspec_to_dict(spec) for spec in parsed.evals],\n    }\n\n\ndef resolve_suite_manifest(suite: str, suites_dir: Path) -> Path:\n    \"\"\"Resolve ``--suite <token>`` to a manifest path on disk.\n\n    Resolution precedence (first match wins):\n\n    1. ``token`` is itself a file path that exists → return it.\n    2. ``suites_dir / f\"{token}.yaml\"`` exists → return it (filename stem\n       convention used by the built-in suites).\n    3. Otherwise scan ``suites_dir`` and return the first manifest whose\n       schema-validated ``name`` field equals ``token``. Broken manifests\n       in the same directory are skipped silently — we only fail on the\n       *requested* suite, not on the neighbours.\n\n    Raises:\n        SuiteNotFound: No manifest matched the token under any rule. CLI\n            callers map this to :data:`SUITE_NOT_FOUND_EXIT_CODE` (= 2).\n    \"\"\"\n\n    candidate = Path(suite)\n    if candidate.is_file():\n        return candidate\n\n    by_filename = suites_dir / f\"{suite}.yaml\"\n    if by_filename.is_file():\n        return by_filename\n\n    loader = SuiteLoader()\n    for manifest_path in discover_suite_manifests(suites_dir):\n        try:\n            parsed = loader.load(manifest_path)\n        except SuiteLoaderError:\n            # Neighbour manifest is broken; don't let it mask the lookup.\n            continue\n        if parsed.name == suite:\n            return manifest_path\n\n    raise SuiteNotFound(suite, suites_dir)\n\n\ndef describe_suite(\n    suite: str,\n    *,\n    suites_dir: Path,\n    eval_id: Optional[str] = None,\n    loader: Optional[SuiteLoader] = None,\n) -> dict:\n    \"\"\"Resolve, load, and project a suite (optionally a single eval).\n\n    Validation runs via :class:`SuiteLoader` BEFORE any rendering, so\n    schema / id-regex / capability rejections raise before the caller\n    can produce stdout (FR-CLI3 AC: validate before print).\n\n    Args:\n        suite: Suite name, filename stem, or direct manifest path. See\n            :func:`resolve_suite_manifest` for resolution precedence.\n        suites_dir: Directory to search when ``suite`` is not a direct\n            path. Tests inject a tmp dir via ``--suites-dir``.\n        eval_id: Optional post-expansion eval id (``E1``, ``E2.1``) to\n            filter the output to a single eval. Missing → ``EvalNotFound``.\n        loader: Injectable :class:`SuiteLoader` for tests that need to\n            stub capability resolution.\n\n    Returns:\n        A dict suitable for ``yaml.safe_dump`` / ``json.dumps``. When\n        ``eval_id`` is ``None``, the dict is the full manifest envelope;\n        when ``eval_id`` is set, the dict is a single ``evalEntry`` shape\n        (the same projection :func:`_evalspec_to_dict` produces).\n\n    Raises:\n        SuiteNotFound: Resolution rule chain produced no manifest.\n        EvalNotFound: ``eval_id`` did not match any post-expansion id.\n        SchemaError / InvalidEvalId / UnresolvedCapability: Propagated\n            from :class:`SuiteLoader` so the CLI exit-code map still\n            applies (all → 2).\n    \"\"\"\n\n    manifest_path = resolve_suite_manifest(suite, suites_dir)\n    active_loader = loader if loader is not None else SuiteLoader()\n    parsed: ParsedSuite = active_loader.load(manifest_path)\n\n    if eval_id is None:\n        return _parsed_suite_to_dict(parsed)\n\n    for spec in parsed.evals:\n        if spec.id == eval_id:\n            return _evalspec_to_dict(spec)\n    raise EvalNotFound(\n        eval_id=eval_id,\n        suite_name=parsed.name,\n        known_ids=sorted(spec.id for spec in parsed.evals),\n    )\n\n\ndef render_describe_yaml(payload: dict) -> str:\n    \"\"\"Render a describe payload as a deterministic YAML document.\n\n    ``sort_keys=False`` preserves the manifest field ordering produced by\n    :func:`_parsed_suite_to_dict` / :func:`_evalspec_to_dict` so the\n    YAML output reads like the source manifest. ``default_flow_style=False``\n    keeps block-style for human review.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    return yaml.safe_dump(\n        payload,\n        sort_keys=False,\n        default_flow_style=False,\n        allow_unicode=True,\n    )\n\n\ndef render_describe_json(payload: dict) -> str:\n    \"\"\"Render a describe payload as a deterministic JSON document.\n\n    ``sort_keys=True`` guarantees byte-level stability across hosts and\n    Python versions (matching the ``eval list --json`` contract).\n    \"\"\"\n\n    return json.dumps(payload, indent=2, sort_keys=True)\n\n\n@eval_group.command(\"describe\")\n@click.option(\n    \"--suite\",\n    \"suite\",\n    required=True,\n    help=(\n        \"Suite name, filename stem, or path to a manifest. Name lookup \"\n        \"matches the manifest 'name:' field; stem lookup matches \"\n        \"'<suites-dir>/<token>.yaml'.\"\n    ),\n)\n@click.option(\n    \"--eval\",\n    \"eval_id\",\n    default=None,\n    help=(\n        \"Filter output to a single post-expansion eval id \"\n        \"(e.g. 'E1' or 'E2.1'). Missing id exits 2 with EvalNotFound.\"\n    ),\n)\n@click.option(\n    \"--json\",\n    \"as_json\",\n    is_flag=True,\n    help=\"Emit JSON instead of YAML (default is YAML).\",\n)\n@click.option(\n    \"--suites-dir\",\n    \"suites_dir\",\n    type=click.Path(file_okay=False, path_type=Path),\n    default=None,\n    help=(\n        \"Override the suite manifest directory used for name/stem \"\n        \"lookups. Defaults to src/superclaude/cli/eval/suites/.\"\n    ),\n)\ndef eval_describe(\n    suite: str,\n    eval_id: Optional[str],\n    as_json: bool,\n    suites_dir: Optional[Path],\n) -> None:\n    \"\"\"Print a validated, post-parameterize-expansion manifest.\n\n    Output mirrors the validated manifest with no editorial\n    transformation. ``evals[]`` is the post-expansion list so a\n    parameterized row produces one entry per row in the output.\n\n    Exit codes:\n\n    * ``0`` — manifest validated and printed.\n    * ``2`` — schema / id-regex / capability rejection, missing suite\n      (``SuiteNotFound``), or missing eval id (``EvalNotFound``). The\n      stderr line names the typed error class so operators can locate\n      the failure without parsing tracebacks.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    target = suites_dir if suites_dir is not None else _DEFAULT_SUITES_DIR\n\n    try:\n        payload = describe_suite(\n            suite,\n            suites_dir=target,\n            eval_id=eval_id,\n        )\n    except SuiteNotFound as exc:\n        click.echo(f\"eval describe: {type(exc).__name__}: {exc}\", err=True)\n        sys.exit(SUITE_NOT_FOUND_EXIT_CODE)\n    except EvalNotFound as exc:\n        click.echo(f\"eval describe: {type(exc).__name__}: {exc}\", err=True)\n        sys.exit(EVAL_NOT_FOUND_EXIT_CODE)\n    except SuiteLoaderError as exc:  # type: ignore[misc]\n        click.echo(\n            f\"eval describe: {type(exc).__name__}: {exc}\",\n            err=True,\n        )\n        sys.exit(SUITE_LOADER_ERROR_EXIT_CODE)\n\n    if as_json:\n        click.echo(render_describe_json(payload))\n    else:\n        # safe_dump terminates with a trailing newline; click.echo adds\n        # another, so strip to keep output tidy and snapshot-stable.\n        click.echo(render_describe_yaml(payload).rstrip(\"\\n\"))\n\n\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n# T04.10 FR-CLI1 ``eval run`` body helpers (D-0072)\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n#\n# Eight module-private helpers consumed by :func:`eval_run`. Manifest authors who write\n   `max_sec=3` mean \"three seconds is allowed,\" and the inclusive\n   reading matches the colloquial intent. The two boundary tests\n   (`test_duration_max_sec_passes_at_boundary`,\n   `test_duration_min_sec_passes_at_boundary`) pin both ends so any\n   future change to strict comparisons fails the suite.\n\n2. **Single-bound missing-bound is informational, not zero/∞.** When\n   only `max_sec` is set, the missing `min_sec` is treated as \"no\n   claim\" rather than \"min_sec=0\" or \"min_sec=-∞\". {"type":"assistant","message":{"model":"claude-opus-4-7","id":"msg_01G5JkmBEgMmEBsXy9PdTgsJ","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"tool_use","id":"toolu_01RALi2iou1qSGa3wV9NvuFJ","name":"Write","input":{"file_path":"/config/workspace/IronClaude/.dev/releases/current/cliEval/artifacts/D-0071/notes.md","content":"# D-0071 — Implementation notes\n\n## Why this task was documentation-only\n\nBy the time T04.09 was reached the surface it pins was already\nin place:\n\n* `eval_group` itself shipped at T01.26 / FR-G3 alongside the\n  top-level `main.add_command(eval_group, name=\"eval\")` line.\n* `doctor`, `list`, and `describe` shipped at T01.13, T01.21, and\n  T01.22 respectively, each bolting onto `eval_group` via\n  `@eval_group.command(...)`.\n* `run` shipped at T04.10 with its 12 flags. The T04.09 acceptance\n  bullets only require `run` to be **reachable** (its `--help`\n  resolves); the body verification is T04.10's responsibility.\n\nSo at T04.09 there was nothing to add to `commands.py` and nothing to\nadd to `main.py`. The deliverable for this task is the test harness\nin `tests/cli/eval/test_eval_group.py` that pins the four-subcommand\nsurface against future regressions, plus the artifacts under\n`D-0071/`.\n\n## Decisions\n\n1. **Assert on the sorted set, not registration order.** Click\n   `--help` rendering sorts subcommands lexicographically, so\n   asserting `set(eval_group.commands) == {\"describe\", \"doctor\",\n   \"list\", \"run\"}` keeps the test resilient to source-file\n   reorderings. Source-order changes are routine (e.g. inserting a\n   new helper between two `@eval_group.command(...)` blocks); the\n   surface contract is what callers depend on.\n\n2. **Pin the entry-point reachability with a second test path.**\n   `test_top_level_main_lists_eval_group` invokes `main, [\"--help\"]`\n   and asserts the word `eval` appears. This is the FR-G3 /\n   T01.26 surface — distinct from the group's own `--help` — and is\n   what regresses when somebody drops the `main.add_command(...)`\n   line. `test_eval_invoked_through_main_lists_subcommands` then\n   asserts dispatch through the entry point reaches the group\n   surface (i.e. `superclaude eval --help` shows all four\n   subcommands), which catches the case where `add_command` is\n   present but the group object is replaced with something missing\n   subcommands.\n\n3. **Defer `run` body verification to T04.10.** T04.09 only asserts\n   `eval run --help` resolves (exit 0) and the word `run` appears in\n   the output. The 12-flag surface, exit-code mapping, and\n   RunOrchestrator wiring are all T04.10 / D-0072 territory.\n\n## Deferred follow-ups\n\n* None. The registration is final; future subcommands (if any) would\n  ship under their own roadmap items and append to this test file.\n\n## Risk notes\n\n* **Click version drift.** Click 8.x is the only supported series for\n  the CLI. The tests use `click.testing.CliRunner` which has been\n  stable across 7.x → 8.x; no version pin is required at the test\n  level beyond the project-wide `pyproject.toml` dependency.\n\n* **Entry-point regression surface.** The single line\n  `main.add_command(eval_group, name=\"eval\")` is the only thing\n  separating \"eval is reachable\" from \"eval is unreachable\".
